# Change pipeline receipts

This file backs `data/changes.json`. It names the surfaces that were extracted from each Claude Code build, the counting rule of each, the ladder of builds read, every candidate the neighbouring-build diffs produced with what became of it, the CHANGELOG hits, and every bisect download. Builds are named by version; offsets are decimal byte offsets into the darwin-arm64 build of that version. It quotes nothing of a build's code: an offset is the whole of the evidence about a build, and a minified name the build invented is named by its offset or described in words (DESIGN, "What is published about the binary"). A change row's provenance offset is the start of the window its site was read in: the name or code the row cites lies within the 700 characters after it, and the one entry carrying `platform` (the Grep and Glob row) names the read itself. The extraction scripts are kept outside this repository.

## Surfaces and counting rules

All six surfaces are read from the JavaScript module text of the build: the modules listed in the Bun standalone module table (the `---- Bun! ----` trailer and its offsets struct), zstd-compressed asset modules skipped. The two oldest builds (1.0.60 and 2.0.0) use an older table layout (a 24-byte offsets struct before the trailer and 36-byte module records), which is read the same way; the `.wasm`, `.node` and `.vsix` modules are skipped. Every extractor fails loudly when its anchor is absent from a build; none failed on any build read.

- **tools**: an object literal carrying, at one brace level, a `name:` key and at least one tool-protocol key (`inputSchema`, `userFacingName`, `isConcurrencySafe`, `mapToolResultToToolResultBlockParam`, `maxResultSizeChars`). The name is a string literal or an identifier resolved to the literal it is bound to (through the ES module import in chunked builds). Change rows are written for PascalCase names and for the snake_case tools built with the client's tool factory (`memory_*`, `self_hosted_runner_*`, `propose_skills`); other lowercase names are MCP server tool listings (computer use, browser, device) or non-tool objects and are listed below as not rows.

- **commands**: an object literal carrying, at one brace level, a `type` key whose value is the string local, local-jsx or prompt (a built-in command) or a `userInvocable:` key (a bundled skill, which a person also types as `/name`), and a `name:` key resolved as above. The names `data_loss`, `unknown_error` and `same-origin` are non-command objects matched by the rule and are excluded.

  The client defines a slash command in four shapes: a plain object literal, an object built by a factory call (an object argument whose `name` key holds the command's name, or a call whose first argument is the command's name as a string, as for output-style in 2.1.168 at 208674107), an entry in the array of another command's `aliases` key, and a bundled skill's `userInvocable` registration. The census above reads the first shape and the fourth, and it reads a factory-built command as absent. Every command row was read again on both bounds for all four shapes. Four rows dated a change of construction and were retired. In 2.0.71 `/review`, `/pr-comments` and `/security-review` are built by a factory whose object arguments begin at 162875978, 162852753 and 162896327, and all three sit in the built-in command array at 163626387. In 2.1.80 `/review` is still factory-built at 178634095. That retires `command-review-removed`, `command-review-added`, `command-pr-comments-removed` and `command-security-review-removed`. Five more rows named a generic loader whose `name:` is a parameter that the census resolved to an unrelated string: the skill-directory loader at 187549096 and the plugin skill loader at 187572789 in 2.1.284, and the MCP skill loader at 207573250 in 2.1.168 and at 208136856 in 2.1.176. No shape defines `/info`, `/running` or `/warn` on either bound, which retires `command-info-added`, `command-info-removed`, `command-running-added`, `command-warn-added` and `command-warn-removed`. In 2.1.211 `bug` is an alias of `/feedback` at 224319879, so `command-bug-added` records `/bug` becoming a command of its own. The candidate table below still lists the diffs these nine rows came from.

- **hook events**: the longest array literal of string literals that contains both `"PreToolUse"` and `"Notification"`.

- **settings keys**: the top-level keys (brace depth 1, no open paren or bracket) of the object literal that carries `cleanupPeriodDays` and `apiKeyHelper` (the largest when several do), plus the keys of the object literal carrying `allow`, `deny` and `defaultMode`, listed as `permissions.<key>`. A key added through a conditional spread is not seen.

- **env**: pass 1 collects every name `[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{2,}` read as a property of `process.env`, plain or bracketed with a string, every name beginning `CLAUDE_CODE_` or `ANTHROPIC_` read as a member of any object (which covers the typed registry's getters and local copies of `process.env`), and every key of the typed env registry (an object literal of fifty or more entries, each binding a variable name to a getter function, at least fifty of the names beginning `CLAUDE_`), in any build; pass 2 takes, per build, the union of pass 1 intersected with the identifier-bounded tokens of that build's module text. A name therefore leaves the set only when the build no longer mentions it at all; a move from a direct read to a registry getter cannot remove it.

- **tengu**: distinct runs of `tengu_[a-z0-9_]+` in module text (the first catalogue pass's rule).

- **aliases** (used to tell a rename from a removal): every `aliases` key holding an array, its elements resolved like names, owned by the nearest `name:` key at the same brace level.

## Candidates and their dispositions

A candidate is a name present in one build and absent in the next build read (or the reverse). Dates follow DESIGN.md: `cached-rung` when the two builds either side were already cached, `bisect` when a downloaded build is one of the two bounds, `changelog` when the official CHANGELOG has an Added/Removed/Renamed/Merged/Unshipped/alias line naming it inside the byte-read interval. A `changelog` row keeps the byte-read interval in the table below.

Not a row, because the name is an MCP server tool listing or a non-tool object matched by the key rule (182 names, every build): `add-dir`, `advisor`, `agents`, `app_ax_find`, `app_batch`, Appearances: 3269 candidates, none a row. A gate or event that appears is dated by the gate pipeline's literal floors.

Disappearances: 786 candidates. Each was classified in the last build that carries it, one pass per build: gate-like when the identifier (or an identifier bound to it nearby) is argument 0 of a call whose argument 1 is a scalar and which closes right after; event-like when argument 1 opens an object or the call has one argument. A gate-like disappearance is a graduation or removal candidate; it becomes a row only when its read site and the code in the next build were read by hand. ### Gate-like disappearances read at both bounds

Each of these 399 identifiers was read in the last cached build that carries it (the read site: the gate-like call, or the one-expression wrapper that makes it, plus the wrapper's callers when it has them) and at the same place in the first cached build without it. The place in the later build was found by voting: every string literal, property name and object key of four or more characters within 900 bytes of the read site that occurs at most three times in the later build votes for the position it implies, and the 300-byte bucket with the most votes is read; where that failed, the replacement was searched by its literals. No build was downloaded; every row is dated cached-rung between the two rungs, or by the CHANGELOG line it quotes when one names the change inside the gap.

Dispositions: **graduated** - the behaviour the gate selected is now unconditional or a constant (for a kill switch, the behaviour it could switch off now always runs); **default changed** - the gate is gone and the unset default moved; **renamed** - the same read site reads another identifier (four rows name an identifier that already existed in the earlier build, and say so); **removed with its feature** - the code the gate turned on is gone, a row only when a person could meet it (a tool, a command, a setting, a visible notice); **event call** - every gate-like call of the identifier is a telemetry call whose second argument is `{}` or an object, judged by the callee: a callee whose `tengu_*` calls carry more object payloads than scalar defaults is the event logger; **read, no row** - the read is recorded but the change has no direction a row can name (the switch moved into an existing variable or config, or the gate decided nothing in that build); **unresolved** - the read site was read but its replacement was not traced (in the chunked builds from 2.1.243 on, short names collide across modules).

Counts: {'default-changed': 1, 'graduated': 145, 'norow-event': 101, 'norow-other': 17, 'norow-removed': 67, 'removed': 20, 'renamed': 25, 'unresolved': 23} (one per identifier).

## What this table does not settle (partial, labelled)

- Dates: 261 of 728 rows are dated to one release (neighbouring builds read, or a CHANGELOG line). The other 467 keep an interval wider than one release: their `enteredAt.version` is the first build read with the new state and `lowerBound` the last build read with the old one. 427 of them span two to ten releases; 40 span more (the 1.0.60 to 2.0.0 and 2.0.0 to 2.0.30 gaps, which no download was spent on). The bisect budget (30 downloads) went to tool and command candidates first.
- Gates: 416 tengu disappearances show a gate-like call, and all 416 were read at both bounds. At the head of the ladder (2.1.284 to 2.1.287) 12 are rows and the five `tengu_dir_sync_*` names read as events; of the other 399, 145 graduated, 1 changed default, 25 were renamed and 20 were removed with a feature a person could meet (each a row), 67 were removed with code nobody meets, 17 were read and have no row for the reason given, 101 turned out to be event calls, and 23 remain unresolved: their read site was read but what replaced it was not traced (listed with the reason). Rows marked whatBasis inferred rest on the code read and the names around it rather than on a traced consumer.
- Tools: a row says a tool object is defined or no longer defined. Whether a session offers a defined tool is decided by its own isEnabled() and by the registry, which these rows do not read; only the Grep and Glob row reads that decision.
- Settings keys added through a conditional spread into the schema object are not seen by the key rule.
- The /ultraplan command is absent from 2.1.90 alone while its usage text and launcher list are present; the two candidates it produced are not rows (extraction miss suspected, UNPROVEN).
- Pre-2.1 history (1.0.60 to 2.0.75) is covered by the same rules but its rows matter less to the current page and most keep cached-rung intervals.

## Bisect

Budget: 30 downloads (darwin-arm64), host downloads.claude.ai only.
Candidates eligible: every tool, command, hook-event and settings-key candidate from the rung diffs whose
interval still holds at least one release between its two rungs and which the official CHANGELOG does
not date by an Added/Removed/Renamed/Merged/Unshipped/alias line inside the interval.
Version universe: versions.json (public list, ends at 2.1.259) union the CHANGELOG headings (2.1.260-2.1.289)
union the cached rungs.
Policy: one download at a time. Each step picks the single version that maximises the summed expected
entropy reduction over every eligible candidate interval that contains it (uniform prior over where in
the interval the change entered), weighted: tool or command removed 3, added 2; hook event 1.5; settings
key removed 2, added 0.5; intervals ending at or before 2.0.30 (pre-2.1) x0.25. A shared gap therefore
gets shared midpoints automatically: one download splits every candidate in that gap.
After each download: sha256 against manifest platforms["darwin-arm64"].checksum; on match the build
joins the cache and the ladder, the five extractors (tools, commands, hooks, settings, tengu) run on it,
the candidates are recomputed, and the step is logged with the candidates it split. A version whose
manifest or binary cannot be fetched or verified is logged and excluded.
Projected first picks (from the pre-download state): 2.1.59 first (inside the 2.1.23..2.1.100 gap,
71 undated candidates); the single-release gaps 2.1.223, 2.1.257, 2.1.260 score high per download and
are expected early; the big pre-2.1.23 gap gets coarse splits.


### Steps

Every download came from the release bucket at `https://downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/<version>/darwin-arm64/claude`; its sha256 was compared with `platforms["darwin-arm64"].checksum` of `<version>/manifest.json` before the build joined the cache.
